decorator to deprecate some or all positional arguments.\\n103: \\n104:     The decorated function will map any positional arguments after the ``*`` to\\n105:     the corresponding keyword arguments and issue a deprecation warning.\\n106: \\n107:     The decorator takes two arguments: a RemovedInDjangoXXWarning warning\\n108:     category and a list of parameter names that have been changed from\\n109:     positional-or-keyword to keyword-only, in their original positional order.\\n110: \\n111:     Works on both functions and methods. To apply to a class constructor,\\n112:     decorate its __init__() method. To apply to a staticmethod or classmethod,\\n113:     use @deprecate_posargs after @staticmethod or @classmethod.\\n114: \\n115:     Example: to deprecate passing option1 or option2 as posargs, change::\\n116: \\n117:         def some_func(request, option1, option2=True):\\n118:             ...\\n119: \\n120:     to::\\n121: \\n122:         @deprecate_posargs(RemovedInDjangoXXWarning, [\\\"option1\\\", \\\"option2\\\"])\\n123:         def some_func(request, *, option1, option2=True):\\n124:             ...\\n125: \\n126:     After the deprecation period, remove the decorator (but keep the ``*``)::\\n127: \\n128:         def some_func(request, *, option1, option2=True):\\n129:             ...\\n130: \\n131:     Caution: during the deprecation period, do not add any new *positional*\\n132:     parameters or change the remaining ones.
